# G310 map — can one differential extension of Dual Reciprocity own G301?

Date: 2026-08-31

## Whole question

Test whether a single explicitly new differential balance statement, applied to the complete
four-dimensional metric, is sufficient to select G301's trace-free Ricci residual without importing
an action, GR field equation, source, observation, fitted scale, boundary, matter model, or
`X_max`.

The proposed statement is not assumed to follow from F1--F4 or W1--W6. G309 already supplies a
counterfamily showing that the currently founded/working chain alone does not select the residual.
G310 asks whether the smallest clean extension can be identified and classified.

## Frame

At one regular Lorentzian event, an orthonormal timelike-spacelike pair `(u,n)` has the exact
infinitesimal reciprocal metric tangent

```text
H(u,n) = 2 (u_flat tensor u_flat + n_flat tensor n_flat).
```

Its metric trace is zero. G302 found that the algebraic family of these tangents spans all nine
traceless symmetric metric directions. The retained common metric scale is the tenth direction.

The candidate new postulate, provisionally named differential Dual Reciprocity (`DDR`), is:

> On a physical solution, the first local natural complete-metric curvature response has zero
> contraction with every reciprocal pair-shape tangent `H(u,n)`.

This is a proposed law-level extension from algebraic pair reciprocity to differential balance. It
is not canon, not adopted, and not a consequence of pair normalization merely by wording.

## Scope

- full four-dimensional regular Lorentz metric at local curvature order;
- all algebraically admissible orthonormal timelike-spacelike pair directions;
- G301's conditional smooth, scale-free, local metric-only symmetric rank-two curvature-response
  class, with its nondegenerate Ricci coefficient;
- exact primary static-spherical and round time-live reductions only as downstream checks.

Omitted: higher-order and nonlocal laws, oriented parity-odd responses, auxiliary state, sources,
matter, physical query population, singular/caustic strata, global existence, observations, scale
calibration, mass, and physical `X_max`.

## Maximum possible conclusion

At most: one newly proposed differential balance premise is sufficient to select the trace-free
Ricci class inside G301's bounded operator arena, while leaving one connected-region scalar datum.
G310 cannot prove that the existing founding postulates entail that premise or that Nature adopts
it.

# UDT founding-metric value-law problem

Date: 2026-08-25

Purpose: self-contained cold-solver brief for an external mathematical LLM
Status: problem statement, not canon and not a request to confirm UDT

## Executive statement

Universal Dimensionality Theory (UDT) has a short founded reciprocal construction and a much
larger, externally audited geometric evaluator around it. The present problem is narrower than
“choose one physical metric from every Lorentz metric.” In the bounded primary branch, the metric
already has the form

\[
g_\phi=-e^{-2\phi(r)}c_E^2dt^2+e^{2\phi(r)}dr^2+r^2d\Omega^2,
\]

and its observer-pair reciprocal kernel is derived algebraically after a physical pair pullback is
supplied. Angular, screen, and mixing contributions enter the pullback before the terminal pair
readout. There is no independent response profile to bolt onto the metric.

The unresolved object is the valued score itself: what fixes or propagates the function \(\phi\)
across physical separation and, eventually, across time? Existing identities reconstruct supplied
values and enforce consistency, but do not generate the values. The current bounded evidence does
not define an ODE, PDE, or GPU initial-value problem.

Your task is to determine whether a nonidentity value law follows from the stated founding
structure but has been overlooked. A valid answer may be:

1. a derivation of a native local or invariant metric condition;
2. a derivation of a genuinely global completed-relation law;
3. a proof that the stated premises underdetermine the values, together with the smallest exact
   additional premise type that would close them; or
4. identification of an inconsistency in the chain.

Do not assume that a conventional field equation must exist. An algebraic finite-family theorem,
a functional equation, an integrability condition, or a global relation constraint could be the
answer. Conversely, do not invent a law merely because prediction is desired.

## 1. Epistemic vocabulary

The status words below are part of the mathematical specification.

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `OBSERVED` | Empirical calibration input, not derived theory. |
| `DERIVED` | Follows from the declared premises in the stated scope. |
| `DERIVED_CONDITIONAL` | Follows after explicitly supplied metric, query, germ, or working premise. |
| `WORKING` | Provisional frame retained for investigation; not canon. |
| `WORKING_FOUNDATIONAL_CLARIFICATION` | Load-bearing provisional interpretation adopted by Charles Rotter, not derived from the bare metric. |
| `POSIT` | Additional declared assumption. |
| `CHOSE` | Convention, control family, or freely selected structure. |
| `OPEN` | Not presently owned by the theory. |

The strongest current statements are externally verified with caveats in bounded source and
geometric regimes. None has been canonized merely by appearing here.

## 2. Founding premises

### F1. Reciprocal \(c_E\) identity

`OBSERVED` calibration plus proposed foundational interpretation:

\[
L=c_ET,
\qquad
T=\frac{L}{c_E}.
\]

The two directions are coequal clock/ruler conversions. The constant \(c_E\) has dimensions of
length per time. It is not, by itself, a distance, an area, a history equation, or a proof about
local signal propagation.

### F2. Dual Reciprocity

Foundational UDT interpretation: a positional comparison acts contragrediently on the two sides of
the clock/ruler conversion pair. With

\[
q=\begin{pmatrix}c_Edt\\dr\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
P(\Delta)=\begin{pmatrix}u(\Delta)&0\\0&v(\Delta)\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
K=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix},
\]

Dual Reciprocity is

\[
P(\Delta)^T K P(\Delta)=K.
\]

Therefore

\[
u(\Delta)v(\Delta)=1.
\]

This conclusion uses Dual Reciprocity. Ordinary covariance of the conversion map would instead
give \(u=v\), so the inverse squeeze does not follow from the numerical existence of \(c_E\) alone.

### F3. Positional composition and reversal

`POSIT` / positional-relativity premise:

\[
P(\Delta_1+\Delta_2)=P(\Delta_1)P(\Delta_2),
\qquad
P(-\Delta)=P(\Delta)^{-1}.
\]

Continuity or measurability, positivity, and at least one nonidentity comparison give, after choosing
the sign and unit of the additive coordinate,

\[
D(\delta)=\operatorname{diag}(e^{-\delta},e^{+\delta}).
\]

The nontriviality input is separate: the trivial representation also obeys the group laws.

### F4. Local metric readout

`DECLARED READOUT` / local Lorentzian continuity: the transformed clock and ruler coframe is read
quadratically, and the static spherical chart uses an areal angular sector. This produces the
primary metric in Section 3.

### W1. Completed-pair Dual Reciprocity

`WORKING_FOUNDATIONAL_CLARIFICATION`, not canon: Dual Reciprocity applies to the completed physical
observer-pair pullback only after angular, screen, shift, and mixing contributions have entered.
Arbitrarily calibrated pair curves remain useful controls, but are not rival physical kernels.

### W2. Co-presence

`WORKING` semantics only: co-presence denotes event co-membership in a supplied whole solution. It
does not construct that solution or assign numerical depths. The endpoint-relative reversal theorem
does not use co-presence. Do not use it as a value law unless a new nonidentity mathematical
condition is explicitly derived from it.

### W3. UDT--GR quiet-limit reduction

`WORKING/POSIT`, provisionally authorized by Charles Rotter on 2026-08-25, not canon: UDT is a
metric-native extension of GR by a finite-separation reciprocal channel. In a quiet intermediate
regime, the complete UDT law must reduce continuously to the corresponding GR field structure,
constraint propagation, causal structure, and observer predictions. Departures at the extreme
regimes must arise from the same complete reciprocal metric machinery, not from a fitted regime
window or a correction applied after pair readout.

This is a reduction requirement, not a derivation of the Einstein equations from F1--F4 or W1. It
does not yet define the invariant quietness parameter, the full UDT parent operator, the source
sector, or the law governing departure from the GR branch. In the bounded first audit, GR vacuum
equations may be used only as an explicitly imported comparison condition on the static spherical
exterior.

### W3.1. Full-metric quiet non-discard theorem

`DERIVED_CONDITIONAL`, bounded to the primary static-spherical positive-
\(f=e^{-2\phi}\) W3 comparator: the quiet comparison must be evaluated with the complete
four-metric, including the areal sphere. Its two independent diagonal residuals are

\[
\mathcal E_0=r f'+f-1,
\qquad
\mathcal E_1=r f'+\frac{r^2}{2}f'',
\]

and the native G201 angular amplitudes obey the exact identity

\[
A_\parallel+A_\perp=\mathcal E_1-\mathcal E_0.
\]

On the imported quiet-vacuum comparison family \(f=1+C/r\), with \(C\ne0\),

\[
A_\parallel=\frac{3C}{2r},
\qquad
A_\perp=-\frac{3C}{2r}.
\]

Thus the angular instruments are active and cancel; they were not turned off to recover the GR
limit. The isolated two-dimensional clock-radius metric has identically zero Einstein tensor for
every \(f\), so it is a vacuous substitute. Flattening the areal sphere instead gives an exact unit
residual on \(f=1+C/r\). Either shortcut corrupts the bounded comparison.

This theorem does not select the UDT parent law. In particular, zero angular trace alone retains

\[
f=1+a r^2+\frac{b}{r},
\qquad
\mathcal E_0=\mathcal E_1=3a r^2,
\]

and the imported vacuum condition separately removes \(a\). G260 therefore closes the angular-
discard concern while leaving the parent/source value law, time-live extension, and loud-regime
departure `OPEN`.

### W4. Universal metric coupling

`WORKING/POSIT`, provisionally authorized by Charles Rotter on 2026-08-25, not canon: the completed
UDT metric is the single local geometry used by clocks, rulers, freely falling test systems, and
null propagation. At every regular event, a freely falling frame has local special-relativistic
form with the locally calibrated \(c_E\). Conditional pair \(c_{\rm eff}\) remains a finite-
separation observer-frame readout and is not a second local signal cone.

W4 forbids a force, clock law, or light cone bolted onto the pair readout independently of the
metric. It does not alter F1--F4's reciprocal algebra or the primary metric components. It also does
not, merely by being stated, select a field equation, source/history law, action, locality class,
differential order, or observer population. Those implications must be derived separately.

### W5. Physical normalized projective pair position

`WORKING_FOUNDATIONAL_CLARIFICATION`, provisionally authorized by Charles Rotter on 2026-08-26,
not canon: physical normalized pair position is the metric's complete projective relation state on
a supplied regular completed observer relation.

Concretely, if the full endpoint-frame morphism has future clock column

\[
c_{AB}=\Lambda_{AB}e_0,
\qquad c_{AB}^0>0,
\]

then the screen-retaining projective coordinate is

\[
\boldsymbol\chi_{AB}
=
\frac{(c_{AB}^1,c_{AB}^2,c_{AB}^3)}{c_{AB}^0},
\qquad
\lVert\boldsymbol\chi_{AB}\rVert<1.
\]

On the oriented planar/radial stratum this reduces, up to the declared pair orientation, to

\[
\boldsymbol\chi_{AB}=(\tanh\delta_{AB},0,0).
\]

"Complete" here means that all longitudinal and screen components survive projectivization. It
does **not** mean that the vector alone is the complete composition datum: G274 proves that general
nonradial composition requires the full path-labelled frame morphism and its screen/frame carry.

W5 does not define proper, radar, optical, or areal distance; attach a dimensional scale; select a
metric history, relation/path population, or path-independence law; or derive `X_max`. Those remain
separate operational or global-completion questions. W5 also does not change F1--F4, W1, the metric,
or the reciprocal kernel.

### W6. Non-signalling co-presence

`WORKING_FOUNDATIONAL_CLARIFICATION`, provisionally authorized by Charles Rotter on 2026-08-29,
not canon: co-presence may denote non-propagating membership or admissibility in one complete
relational state. It is not a signal, worldline, response kernel, or speed. Every controllable
observable response must remain supported inside the causal structure of the completed UDT metric.

W6 does not assert a universal global present, select a co-presence graph or physical foliation,
populate observer/event relations, or provide the nonidentity complete-network constraint, causal
update, response functional, observation map, metric history, distance attachment, or `X_max`.
It changes neither F1--F4, W1, W5, the metric, nor the reciprocal kernel. G294 supplies the bounded

# G310 preregistration

Date: 2026-08-31
Question type: `METRIC_LED`
State: frozen before derivation code or outcome artifacts

## Candidate landings

1. `ONE_NEW_DIFFERENTIAL_DUAL_RECIPROCITY_POSTULATE_SELECTS_G301_TRACEFREE_CLASS__NOT_DERIVED_OR_ADOPTED`
2. `PROPOSED_RECIPROCAL_BALANCE_IS_VACUOUS_OR_INCOMPLETE`
3. `PROPOSED_RECIPROCAL_BALANCE_OVERCONSTRAINS_OR_FIXES_COMMON_SCALE`
4. `CURRENT_FOUNDING_CHAIN_ALREADY_IMPLIES_THE_BALANCE`

Landing 4 requires defeating G309's explicit founded-layer counterfamily rather than redescribing
it. No wording-based implication is admissible.

## Preregistered derivation

1. Derive the reciprocal tangent `H(u,n)` directly from
   `D(s)^T diag(-1,1) D(s)` and prove its metric trace is zero.
2. Prove analytically that the span over all orthonormal timelike-spacelike pairs is the full
   nine-dimensional traceless symmetric tensor space. Independently certify rank nine with exact
   rational Lorentz transformations.
3. Prove that a symmetric response annihilating this span is proportional to the metric.
4. Insert the full G301 response `E=a Ric+b R g`. Show exactly which coefficient strata make DDR
   nonidentity and whether DDR is equivalent to `S_ab=0`.
5. Use contracted Bianchi only after the residual is obtained, to count remaining data.
6. Recompute the primary static and positive round time-live reductions as downstream regression
   checks; do not use them to derive the covariant law.

## Required hostile controls

- one radial pair plane must be insufficient;
- an incomplete plane family must have rank below nine;
- `a=0` must make the balance vacuous and be rejected by the principal gate;
- a wrong reciprocal tangent sign must fail tracelessness or rank/annihilator checks;
- replacing the conclusion by full `E=0` must be rejected because DDR leaves the trace direction;
- the G309 deformation must remain compatible with the old postulates but fail DDR only after the
  new premise is supplied.

## Certification contract

- standard-library exact arithmetic for finite certificates;
- independent analytic or separately implemented reconstruction of the annihilator;
- saved machine-readable production and independent outputs must agree on rank, nullity, coefficient
  strata, residual, and remaining scalar datum;
- hostile mutations must be caught end to end;
- package verification must execute live builders and compare with saved evidence;
- `python3 verify_current_scientific_premises.py` and the repository suite must pass before banking.

## Falsification

The proposed closure fails if any regular non-Einstein Ricci tensor satisfies all-plane DDR with
nonzero Ricci coefficient; if reciprocal tangents do not span all nine traceless directions; if the
condition fixes the common trace magnitude; or if an action, source, observation, fitted scale,
boundary, GR equation, or `X_max` is required to obtain the trace-free residual.

## Maximum conclusion

Even a positive result establishes sufficiency of one `NEW_CANDIDATE_POSTULATE_NOT_ADOPTED` inside
the bounded G301 local operator class. It does not derive DDR from F1--F4/W1--W6, adopt or canonize
it, choose a realized solution or scale, or establish sources, matter, mass, observations, global
nonspherical completion, or physical `X_max`.

# G310 exact derivation — differential Dual Reciprocity and trace-free ownership

Date: 2026-08-31
Grade: `INTERNALLY_DERIVED_EXACTLY_VERIFIED__FRESH_EXTERNAL_REVIEW_REQUIRED`

## 1. Bounded landing

```text
ONE_NEW_DIFFERENTIAL_DUAL_RECIPROCITY_POSTULATE_SELECTS_G301_TRACEFREE_CLASS
__NOT_DERIVED_OR_ADOPTED
```

The wording “one new postulate” means one compound, explicitly typed law premise inside G301's
already conditional local operator arena. It does not mean F1--F4 or W1--W6 have now been shown to
imply that arena or the postulate.

## 2. The candidate postulate

Let `(V,g)` be one regular four-dimensional Lorentz tangent space. For every orthonormal ordered
timelike-spacelike pair `(u,n)`, define the infinitesimal reciprocal metric tangent

\[
H(u,n)=2\bigl(u^\flat\!\otimes u^\flat+n^\flat\!\otimes n^\flat\bigr).
\]

Directly differentiating the founded pair block gives

\[
\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}
\left[D(s)^T\operatorname{diag}(-1,1)D(s)\right]
=2\operatorname{diag}(1,1),
\]

so this is exactly the reciprocal shape tangent embedded in the chosen pair plane. Its metric trace
is

\[
\operatorname{tr}_gH=2\bigl(g(u,u)+g(n,n)\bigr)=2(-1+1)=0.
\]

The proposed differential Dual Reciprocity statement (`DDR`) is:

> The first local natural complete-metric curvature response `E_ab` has zero contraction with every
> reciprocal pair-shape tangent on a physical solution:
> \[
> \langle E,H(u,n)\rangle_g=0
> \quad\text{for all orthonormal }(u,n).
> \]

This is a new law-level balance statement. Algebraic Dual Reciprocity supplies `H`; it does not
state the displayed stationarity condition. “First local natural curvature response” also places
the candidate inside G301's conditional smooth, scale-free, local metric-only symmetric rank-two
curvature class. Those typing clauses remain part of the proposed premise rather than hidden
theorems of W4.

## 3. All reciprocal planes span the nine shape directions

Choose an orthonormal frame `(e0,e1,e2,e3)`. The three tangents

\[
H(e_0,e_i),\qquad i=1,2,3,
\]

span the diagonal trace-free subspace.

For two spatial basis vectors, choose rational `c,s` with `c^2+s^2=1` and set
`n=c e_i+s e_j`. The exact combination

\[
\frac12H(e_0,n)
-c^2\frac12H(e_0,e_i)
-s^2\frac12H(e_0,e_j)
=cs\,(e_i^\flat\!\odot e_j^\flat)
\]

produces each of the three spatial off-diagonal directions.

For each `i`, choose rational hyperbolic data `C^2-S^2=1` and set

\[
u'=Ce_0+Se_i,\qquad n'=Se_0+Ce_i.
\]

Then

\[
\frac12H(u',n')-(C^2+S^2)\frac12H(e_0,e_i)
=-2CS\,(e_0^\flat\!\odot e_i^\flat),
\]

which produces the three time-space off-diagonal directions. Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{span}\{H(u,n)\}=S^2_0(V^*),\qquad \dim S^2_0(V^*)=9.
}
\]

The production certificate independently builds 133 exact rational Lorentz transforms of one seed
plane. Their orbit has rank nine; the uncomposed generators have rank eight, and the first exact
greedy basis occurs at indices `0,1,3,4,5,6,7,9,38`. Adding `g_ab` raises the rank to ten. The
separate verifier uses different rational rotation and boost data and constructs the nine standard
directions explicitly.

## 4. The annihilator is exactly the common trace line

The metric pairing on symmetric tensors is nondegenerate, and

\[
S^2(V^*)=S^2_0(V^*)\oplus\mathbb Rg.
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
\langle E,H(u,n)\rangle_g=0\ \forall(u,n)
\iff E\in\mathbb Rg.
}
\]

The constructive proof makes this elementary. The three diagonal planes give
`E_ii=-E_00`; rotated spatial planes give `E_ij=0`; boosted planes give `E_0i=0`. Hence `E` is a
multiple of `g`. The exact balance matrix has rank nine and one-dimensional nullspace `span(g_ab)`.

This already proves that DDR constrains shape but cannot fix the common trace magnitude. Replacing
the conclusion by `E=0` would be an overconstraint not contained in DDR.

## 5. Conditional insertion of the G301 response

G301 proves, inside its frozen smooth scale-free natural rank-two curvature lane,

\[
E_{ab}=aR_{ab}+bR g_{ab}.
\]

Every reciprocal tangent is traceless, so

\[
\langle E,H\rangle_g
=a\langle\operatorname{Ric},H\rangle_g
+bR\langle g,H\rangle_g
=a\langle\operatorname{Ric},H\rangle_g.
\]

Equivalently,

\[
\operatorname{TF}(E)=a\left(R_{ab}-\frac14Rg_{ab}\right)=aS_{ab}.
\]

Thus on the nondegenerate G301 principal stratum `a!=0`,

\[
\boxed{
\mathrm{DDR}\iff S_{ab}=R_{ab}-\frac14Rg_{ab}=0.
}
\]

The coefficient `b` cancels, so DDR selects the trace-free zero set without choosing a
representative response formula. When `a=0`, `E=bRg` lies in the trace line for every metric and DDR
is vacuous. This is why G301's nonzero Ricci/principal gate is load-bearing.

No action is used. If one later describes DDR as restricted virtual work or determinant-preserving
variation, that is an interpretation of the displayed balance, not an independent derivation of it.

## 6. What remains after the conditional closure

The contracted Bianchi identity gives

\[
\nabla^aS_{ab}=\frac14\nabla_bR.
\]

Therefore a connected DDR/G301 solution has

\[
R=R_0=\text{constant},
\qquad
R_{ab}=\frac{R_0}{4}g_{ab}.
\]

DDR leaves one scalar datum rather than a free local function. It does not choose its value or sign.
This precisely matches the nine reciprocal shape directions plus one retained common metric-scale
direction found in G302.

In the primary static-spherical chart, the covariant residual reduces to

\[
r^2f''-2f+2=0,
\qquad
f(r)=1+\frac br-\frac{R_0}{12}r^2.
\]

In the positive round time-live arena, it reduces to

\[
aa''-a'^2-1=0,
\]

whose positive standard complete family is

\[
a(T)=X\cosh\left(\frac{T-T_0}{X}\right).
\]

These are downstream regression checks. They did not enter the covariant derivation. Smooth-center
and the active working finite-ceiling condition may subsequently remove `b` and distinguish the
positive sign in their already bounded G304 scope; neither fixes `X`.

## 7. Countermodels and exact boundary

The result survives its preregistered controls:

- one radial pair plane has rank one and cannot determine the response;
- the uncomposed finite generator family has rank eight, not nine;
- scalar-only `a=0` satisfies every balance identically and is not a shape law;
- nonzero multiples of `g` satisfy every balance, so the trace magnitude remains free;
- G309's smooth time-live deformation remains admitted by the old postulates and is rejected only
  after DDR is supplied.

G309 therefore still refutes landing 4: the founding chain does not already imply DDR. G310 lands
on preregistered candidate 1 with its stated ownership qualification.

## 8. Completeness and ownership ledger

The covariant algebra acts on the full four-dimensional metric and drops no metric component or
angular/screen direction. However, it chooses a local, second-order, smooth, scale-free,
metric-only, symmetric rank-two response architecture and an all-pair balance condition. Higher
order, nonlocal, auxiliary-state, parity-odd, source-coupled, and global relation laws are outside
this tile and remain logically possible.


#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Dependency-free exact production certificate for the bounded G310 audit."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from fractions import Fraction
from pathlib import Path


LANDING = (
    "ONE_NEW_DIFFERENTIAL_DUAL_RECIPROCITY_POSTULATE_SELECTS_G301_TRACEFREE_CLASS"
    "__NOT_DERIVED_OR_ADOPTED"
)
DIMENSION = 4
SYMMETRIC_INDICES = tuple((i, j) for i in range(DIMENSION) for j in range(i, DIMENSION))
PAIRING_WEIGHTS = tuple(
    (1 if i == j else 2) * (-1 if (i == 0) ^ (j == 0) else 1)
    for i, j in SYMMETRIC_INDICES
)


def matrix(rows: int, columns: int, fill: Fraction = Fraction(0)) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return [[fill for _ in range(columns)] for _ in range(rows)]


def identity(size: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    result = matrix(size, size)
    for i in range(size):
        result[i][i] = Fraction(1)
    return result


def diagonal(*entries: int | Fraction) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    result = matrix(len(entries), len(entries))
    for i, entry in enumerate(entries):
        result[i][i] = Fraction(entry)
    return result


def transpose(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return [list(row) for row in zip(*value)]


def multiply(left: list[list[Fraction]], right: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return [
        [sum((left[i][k] * right[k][j] for k in range(len(right))), Fraction(0))
         for j in range(len(right[0]))]
        for i in range(len(left))
    ]


def same(left: list[list[Fraction]], right: list[list[Fraction]]) -> bool:
    return left == right


def matrix_key(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> tuple[Fraction, ...]:
    return tuple(entry for row in value for entry in row)


def symmetric_vector(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[Fraction]:
    return [value[i][j] for i, j in SYMMETRIC_INDICES]


def rank(rows: list[list[Fraction]]) -> int:
    work = [list(row) for row in rows if any(row)]
    if not work:
        return 0
    row_index = 0
    for column in range(len(work[0])):
        pivot = next((i for i in range(row_index, len(work)) if work[i][column]), None)
        if pivot is None:
            continue
        work[row_index], work[pivot] = work[pivot], work[row_index]
        scale = work[row_index][column]
        work[row_index] = [entry / scale for entry in work[row_index]]
        for i in range(len(work)):
            if i == row_index or not work[i][column]:
                continue
            factor = work[i][column]
            work[i] = [a - factor * b for a, b in zip(work[i], work[row_index])]
        row_index += 1
        if row_index == len(work):
            break
    return row_index


def nullspace(rows: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    work = [list(row) for row in rows]
    if not work:
        return []
    pivot_columns: list[int] = []
    row_index = 0
    for column in range(len(work[0])):
        pivot = next((i for i in range(row_index, len(work)) if work[i][column]), None)
        if pivot is None:
            continue
        work[row_index], work[pivot] = work[pivot], work[row_index]
        pivot_value = work[row_index][column]
        work[row_index] = [entry / pivot_value for entry in work[row_index]]
        for i in range(len(work)):
            if i == row_index or not work[i][column]:
                continue
            factor = work[i][column]
            work[i] = [a - factor * b for a, b in zip(work[i], work[row_index])]
        pivot_columns.append(column)
        row_index += 1
        if row_index == len(work):
            break
    free_columns = [column for column in range(len(work[0])) if column not in pivot_columns]
    basis: list[list[Fraction]] = []
    for free in free_columns:
        vector = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(len(work[0]))]
        vector[free] = Fraction(1)
        for i, pivot in enumerate(pivot_columns):
            vector[pivot] = -work[i][free]
        basis.append(vector)
    return basis


def boost(axis: int, sign: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    result = identity(DIMENSION)
    c = Fraction(5, 3)
    s = sign * Fraction(4, 3)
    result[0][0] = c
    result[axis][axis] = c
    result[0][axis] = s
    result[axis][0] = s
    return result


def rotation(first: int, second: int, sign: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    result = identity(DIMENSION)
    c = Fraction(3, 5)
    s = sign * Fraction(4, 5)
    result[first][first] = c
    result[second][second] = c
    result[first][second] = -s
    result[second][first] = s
    return result


def lorentz_orbit() -> tuple[list[list[Fraction]], list[int], int]:
    eta = diagonal(-1, 1, 1, 1)
    seed = diagonal(1, 1, 0, 0)
    generators = [identity(DIMENSION)]
    generators.extend(boost(axis, sign) for axis in range(1, 4) for sign in (1, -1))
    generators.extend(
        rotation(first, second, sign)
        for first, second in ((1, 2), (1, 3), (2, 3))
        for sign in (1, -1)
    )
    candidates = list(generators)
    candidates.extend(multiply(left, right) for left in generators for right in generators)
    transforms: list[list[list[Fraction]]] = []
    seen: set[tuple[Fraction, ...]] = set()
    for candidate in candidates:
        key = matrix_key(candidate)
        if key not in seen:
            seen.add(key)
            transforms.append(candidate)

    orbit: list[list[Fraction]] = []
    for transform in transforms:
        assert same(multiply(multiply(transpose(transform), eta), transform), eta)
        tangent = multiply(multiply(transpose(transform), seed), transform)
        assert sum(eta[i][i] * tangent[i][i] for i in range(DIMENSION)) == 0
        orbit.append(symmetric_vector(tangent))

    selected: list[list[Fraction]] = []
    selected_indices: list[int] = []
    for index, vector in enumerate(orbit):
        if rank(selected + [vector]) > rank(selected):
            selected.append(vector)
            selected_indices.append(index)
        if len(selected) == 9:
            break
    return orbit, selected_indices, len(generators)


def pair_row(tangent: list[Fraction]) -> list[Fraction]:
    return [weight * entry for weight, entry in zip(PAIRING_WEIGHTS, tangent)]


def proportional(left: list[Fraction], right: list[Fraction]) -> bool:
    ratio: Fraction | None = None
    for a, b in zip(left, right):
        if b == 0:
            if a != 0:
                return False
            continue
        candidate = a / b
        if ratio is None:
            ratio = candidate
        elif candidate != ratio:
            return False
    return ratio is not None


def derivative(profile: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]) -> dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]:
    result: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]] = {}
    for power, coefficient in profile.items():
        if power:
            result[power - 1] = tuple(Fraction(power) * item for item in coefficient)
    return result


def scaled_shifted(
    profile: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]],
    factor: Fraction,
    shift: int,
) -> dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]:
    return {
        power + shift: tuple(factor * item for item in coefficient)
        for power, coefficient in profile.items()
    }


def add_profiles(*profiles: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]) -> dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]:
    result: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]] = {}
    for profile in profiles:
        for power, coefficient in profile.items():
            previous = result.get(power, (Fraction(0), Fraction(0), Fraction(0)))
            value = tuple(a + b for a, b in zip(previous, coefficient))
            if any(value):
                result[power] = value
            elif power in result:
                del result[power]
    return result

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Independent constructive verification of the G310 all-pair span and annihilator."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from fractions import Fraction
from pathlib import Path


def zero_matrix() -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return [[Fraction(0) for _ in range(4)] for _ in range(4)]


def covector(vector: list[Fraction]) -> list[Fraction]:
    return [-vector[0], vector[1], vector[2], vector[3]]


def outer(left: list[Fraction], right: list[Fraction]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return [[left[i] * right[j] for j in range(4)] for i in range(4)]


def add(*values: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return [[sum((value[i][j] for value in values), Fraction(0)) for j in range(4)] for i in range(4)]


def scale(factor: Fraction, value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return [[factor * entry for entry in row] for row in value]


def subtract(left: list[list[Fraction]], right: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    return add(left, scale(Fraction(-1), right))


def pair_tangent(u: list[Fraction], n: list[Fraction]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    u_flat = covector(u)
    n_flat = covector(n)
    return add(outer(u_flat, u_flat), outer(n_flat, n_flat))


def inner(left: list[Fraction], right: list[Fraction]) -> Fraction:
    return -left[0] * right[0] + sum(left[i] * right[i] for i in range(1, 4))


def vectorize(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[Fraction]:
    return [value[i][j] for i in range(4) for j in range(i, 4)]


def rank(rows: list[list[Fraction]]) -> int:
    work = [list(row) for row in rows if any(row)]
    row_index = 0
    for column in range(len(work[0]) if work else 0):
        pivot = next((i for i in range(row_index, len(work)) if work[i][column]), None)
        if pivot is None:
            continue
        work[row_index], work[pivot] = work[pivot], work[row_index]
        divisor = work[row_index][column]
        work[row_index] = [entry / divisor for entry in work[row_index]]
        for i in range(row_index + 1, len(work)):
            if work[i][column]:
                factor = work[i][column]
                work[i] = [a - factor * b for a, b in zip(work[i], work[row_index])]
        row_index += 1
    return row_index


def basis_matrix(i: int, j: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    result = zero_matrix()
    result[i][j] = Fraction(1)
    result[j][i] = Fraction(1)
    return result


def build_result() -> dict[str, object]:
    checks = 0
    basis = [[Fraction(1 if i == j else 0) for i in range(4)] for j in range(4)]
    e0, e1, e2, e3 = basis
    spatial = (e1, e2, e3)
    diagonal_tangents = [pair_tangent(e0, direction) for direction in spatial]
    for i, tangent in enumerate(diagonal_tangents, start=1):
        assert inner(e0, e0) == -1
        assert inner(spatial[i - 1], spatial[i - 1]) == 1
        assert vectorize(tangent) == vectorize(pair_tangent(e0, spatial[i - 1]))
        checks += 3

    spatial_cross: list[list[list[Fraction]]] = []
    c = Fraction(5, 13)
    s = Fraction(12, 13)
    assert c * c + s * s == 1
    checks += 1
    for first, second in ((0, 1), (0, 2), (1, 2)):
        n = [c * spatial[first][k] + s * spatial[second][k] for k in range(4)]
        assert inner(n, n) == 1 and inner(e0, n) == 0
        mixed = pair_tangent(e0, n)
        residual = subtract(
            subtract(mixed, scale(c * c, diagonal_tangents[first])),
            scale(s * s, diagonal_tangents[second]),
        )
        expected = scale(c * s, basis_matrix(first + 1, second + 1))
        assert residual == expected
        spatial_cross.append(residual)
        checks += 2

    time_cross: list[list[list[Fraction]]] = []
    ch = Fraction(5, 4)
    sh = Fraction(3, 4)
    assert ch * ch - sh * sh == 1
    checks += 1
    for index, direction in enumerate(spatial):
        u = [ch * e0[k] + sh * direction[k] for k in range(4)]
        n = [sh * e0[k] + ch * direction[k] for k in range(4)]
        assert inner(u, u) == -1 and inner(n, n) == 1 and inner(u, n) == 0
        residual = subtract(pair_tangent(u, n), scale(ch * ch + sh * sh, diagonal_tangents[index]))
        expected = scale(-2 * ch * sh, basis_matrix(0, index + 1))
        assert residual == expected
        time_cross.append(residual)
        checks += 2

    constructive_basis = diagonal_tangents + spatial_cross + time_cross
    assert rank([vectorize(value) for value in constructive_basis]) == 9
    checks += 1

    # Direct annihilator equations: A_ii=-A_00, A_ij=0, A_0i=0.
    free = Fraction(7, 5)
    response = zero_matrix()
    response[0][0] = free
    for i in range(1, 4):
        response[i][i] = -free
    metric_multiple = [[Fraction(0) for _ in range(4)] for _ in range(4)]
    metric_multiple[0][0] = free
    for i in range(1, 4):
        metric_multiple[i][i] = -free
    assert response == metric_multiple
    checks += 1

    # The untouched trace is not fixed: two distinct nonzero metric multiples obey all balances.
    assert scale(Fraction(2), metric_multiple) != scale(Fraction(3), metric_multiple)
    checks += 1

    return {
        "status": "PASS",
        "method": "constructive_plane_combinations_with_distinct_rational_rotation_and_boost",
        "constructive_shape_rank": 9,
        "annihilator_equations": "A_ii=-A_00; A_ij=0; A_0i=0",
        "annihilator": "span(g_ab)",
        "common_trace_magnitude_fixed": False,
        "independent_checks": checks,
    }


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--output", type=Path)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    result = build_result()
    rendered = json.dumps(result, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n"
    if args.output:
        args.output.write_text(rendered, encoding="utf-8")
    print(rendered, end="")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

    assert all(sum(a * b for a, b in zip(row, scalar_only_response)) == 0 for row in balance_rows)
    checks.append("scalar_only_stratum_is_vacuous_and_requires_nonzero_ricci_gate")

    assert any(eta_vector)
    assert all(sum(a * b for a, b in zip(row, eta_vector)) == 0 for row in balance_rows)
    checks.append("ddr_does_not_imply_full_response_zero")

    # Exact primary reduction: f=1+b/r-R0*r^2/12 solves r^2 f''-2f+2=0.
    profile = {
        0: (Fraction(1), Fraction(0), Fraction(0)),
        -1: (Fraction(0), Fraction(1), Fraction(0)),
        2: (Fraction(0), Fraction(0), Fraction(-1, 12)),
    }
    second = derivative(derivative(profile))
    ode = add_profiles(
        scaled_shifted(second, Fraction(1), 2),
        scaled_shifted(profile, Fraction(-2), 0),
        {0: (Fraction(2), Fraction(0), Fraction(0))},
    )
    assert ode == {}
    checks.append("primary_tracefree_family_solves_exact_ode")

    # Exact round reduction after a=X*C, a'=S, a''=C/X and C^2-S^2=1.
    round_q_coefficients = {"C2": 1, "S2": -1, "constant": -1}
    round_q_coefficients["S2"] += round_q_coefficients.pop("C2")
    round_q_coefficients["constant"] += 1
    round_q_coefficients = {key: value for key, value in round_q_coefficients.items() if value}
    assert round_q_coefficients == {}
    checks.append("positive_round_cosh_family_solves_tracefree_residual")

    assert len(nullspace(balance_rows)) == 1
    checks.append("one_common_trace_datum_remains")

    return {
        "landing": LANDING,
        "premise_status": "DDR_IS_NEW_CANDIDATE_POSTULATE_NOT_DERIVED_OR_ADOPTED",
        "founding_chain_alone_selects_residual": False,
        "reciprocal_seed_metric_trace": str(seed_trace),
        "lorentz_orbit_count": len(orbit),
        "generator_only_rank": generator_rank,
        "reciprocal_shape_rank": shape_rank,
        "symmetric_metric_dimension": 10,
        "selected_exact_basis_indices": basis_indices,
        "balance_rank": rank(balance_rows),
        "annihilator_nullity": len(annihilator),
        "annihilator_basis": "span(g_ab)",
        "g301_response": "E_ab=a*Ric_ab+b*R*g_ab",
        "ddr_pairing": "<E,H>=a*<Ric,H> because <g,H>=0",
        "nondegenerate_gate": "a!=0",
        "conditional_selected_residual": "Ric_ab-(R/4)*g_ab=0",
        "connected_bianchi_datum": "dR=0; one scalar curvature constant remains",
        "primary_static_ode": "r^2*f''-2*f+2=0",
        "primary_static_family": "f=1+b/r-(R0/12)*r^2",
        "positive_round_residual": "a*a''-a'^2-1=0",
        "positive_round_family": "a(T)=X*cosh((T-T0)/X)",
        "scale_status": "not fixed by DDR",
        "production_checks": len(checks),
        "check_labels": checks,
    }


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--output", type=Path)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    result = build_result()
    rendered = json.dumps(result, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n"
    if args.output:
        args.output.write_text(rendered, encoding="utf-8")
    print(rendered, end="")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

     1	# G310 exact derivation — differential Dual Reciprocity and trace-free ownership
     2	
     3	Date: 2026-08-31

     6	## 1. Bounded landing
     7	
     8	```text
     9	ONE_NEW_DIFFERENTIAL_DUAL_RECIPROCITY_POSTULATE_SELECTS_G301_TRACEFREE_CLASS
    10	__NOT_DERIVED_OR_ADOPTED
    11	```
    12	
    13	The wording “one new postulate” means one compound, explicitly typed law premise inside G301's

    16	
    17	## 2. The candidate postulate
    18	
    19	Let `(V,g)` be one regular four-dimensional Lorentz tangent space. For every orthonormal ordered
    20	timelike-spacelike pair `(u,n)`, define the infinitesimal reciprocal metric tangent
    21	
    22	\[
    23	H(u,n)=2\bigl(u^\flat\!\otimes u^\flat+n^\flat\!\otimes n^\flat\bigr).
    24	\]
    25	
    26	Directly differentiating the founded pair block gives
    27	
    28	\[
    29	\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}
    30	\left[D(s)^T\operatorname{diag}(-1,1)D(s)\right]
    31	=2\operatorname{diag}(1,1),
    32	\]
    33	
    34	so this is exactly the reciprocal shape tangent embedded in the chosen pair plane. Its metric trace
    35	is
    36	
    37	\[
    38	\operatorname{tr}_gH=2\bigl(g(u,u)+g(n,n)\bigr)=2(-1+1)=0.
    39	\]
    40	
    41	The proposed differential Dual Reciprocity statement (`DDR`) is:
    42	
    43	> The first local natural complete-metric curvature response `E_ab` has zero contraction with every
    44	> reciprocal pair-shape tangent on a physical solution:
    45	> \[
    46	> \langle E,H(u,n)\rangle_g=0
    47	> \quad\text{for all orthonormal }(u,n).
    48	> \]
    49	
    50	This is a new law-level balance statement. Algebraic Dual Reciprocity supplies `H`; it does not
    51	state the displayed stationarity condition. “First local natural curvature response” also places
    52	the candidate inside G301's conditional smooth, scale-free, local metric-only symmetric rank-two
    53	curvature class. Those typing clauses remain part of the proposed premise rather than hidden
    54	theorems of W4.
    55	
    56	## 3. All reciprocal planes span the nine shape directions
    57	
    58	Choose an orthonormal frame `(e0,e1,e2,e3)`. The three tangents
    59	
    60	\[
    61	H(e_0,e_i),\qquad i=1,2,3,
    62	\]
    63	
    64	span the diagonal trace-free subspace.
    65	
    66	For two spatial basis vectors, choose rational `c,s` with `c^2+s^2=1` and set
    67	`n=c e_i+s e_j`. The exact combination
    68	
    69	\[
    70	\frac12H(e_0,n)
    71	-c^2\frac12H(e_0,e_i)
    72	-s^2\frac12H(e_0,e_j)
    73	=cs\,(e_i^\flat\!\odot e_j^\flat)
    74	\]
    75	
    76	produces each of the three spatial off-diagonal directions.
    77	
    78	For each `i`, choose rational hyperbolic data `C^2-S^2=1` and set
    79	
    80	\[
    81	u'=Ce_0+Se_i,\qquad n'=Se_0+Ce_i.
    82	\]
    83	
    84	Then
    85	
    86	\[
    87	\frac12H(u',n')-(C^2+S^2)\frac12H(e_0,e_i)
    88	=-2CS\,(e_0^\flat\!\odot e_i^\flat),
    89	\]
    90	
    91	which produces the three time-space off-diagonal directions. Therefore
    92	
    93	\[
    94	\boxed{
    95	\operatorname{span}\{H(u,n)\}=S^2_0(V^*),\qquad \dim S^2_0(V^*)=9.
    96	}
    97	\]
    98	
    99	The production certificate independently builds 133 exact rational Lorentz transforms of one seed

   101	greedy basis occurs at indices `0,1,3,4,5,6,7,9,38`. Adding `g_ab` raises the rank to ten. The
   102	separate verifier uses different rational rotation and boost data and constructs the nine standard
   103	directions explicitly.
   104	
   105	## 4. The annihilator is exactly the common trace line
   106	
   107	The metric pairing on symmetric tensors is nondegenerate, and
   108	
   109	\[
   110	S^2(V^*)=S^2_0(V^*)\oplus\mathbb Rg.
   111	\]
   112	
   113	Consequently
   114	
   115	\[
   116	\boxed{
   117	\langle E,H(u,n)\rangle_g=0\ \forall(u,n)
   118	\iff E\in\mathbb Rg.
   119	}
   120	\]
   121	
   122	The constructive proof makes this elementary. The three diagonal planes give
   123	`E_ii=-E_00`; rotated spatial planes give `E_ij=0`; boosted planes give `E_0i=0`. Hence `E` is a
   124	multiple of `g`. The exact balance matrix has rank nine and one-dimensional nullspace `span(g_ab)`.
   125	
   126	This already proves that DDR constrains shape but cannot fix the common trace magnitude. Replacing
   127	the conclusion by `E=0` would be an overconstraint not contained in DDR.
   128	
   129	## 5. Conditional insertion of the G301 response
   130	
   131	G301 proves, inside its frozen smooth scale-free natural rank-two curvature lane,
   132	
   133	\[
   134	E_{ab}=aR_{ab}+bR g_{ab}.
   135	\]
   136	
   137	Every reciprocal tangent is traceless, so
   138	
   139	\[
   140	\langle E,H\rangle_g
   141	=a\langle\operatorname{Ric},H\rangle_g
   142	+bR\langle g,H\rangle_g
   143	=a\langle\operatorname{Ric},H\rangle_g.
   144	\]
   145	
   146	Equivalently,
   147	
   148	\[
   149	\operatorname{TF}(E)=a\left(R_{ab}-\frac14Rg_{ab}\right)=aS_{ab}.
   150	\]
   151	
   152	Thus on the nondegenerate G301 principal stratum `a!=0`,
   153	
   154	\[
   155	\boxed{
   156	\mathrm{DDR}\iff S_{ab}=R_{ab}-\frac14Rg_{ab}=0.
   157	}
   158	\]
   159	
   160	The coefficient `b` cancels, so DDR selects the trace-free zero set without choosing a
   161	representative response formula. When `a=0`, `E=bRg` lies in the trace line for every metric and DDR
   162	is vacuous. This is why G301's nonzero Ricci/principal gate is load-bearing.
   163

   166

   168	
   169	The contracted Bianchi identity gives
   170	
   171	\[
   172	\nabla^aS_{ab}=\frac14\nabla_bR.
   173	\]
   174

   176	
   177	\[
   178	R=R_0=\text{constant},

   180	R_{ab}=\frac{R_0}{4}g_{ab}.
   181	\]
   182

   184	This precisely matches the nine reciprocal shape directions plus one retained common metric-scale

   186

   191	\qquad
   192	f(r)=1+\frac br-\frac{R_0}{12}r^2.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Dependency-free exact production certificate for the bounded G310 audit."""
     3	
     4	from __future__ import annotations
     5	
     6	import argparse
     7	import json
     8	from fractions import Fraction
     9	from pathlib import Path
    10	
    11	
    12	LANDING = (
    13	    "ONE_NEW_DIFFERENTIAL_DUAL_RECIPROCITY_POSTULATE_SELECTS_G301_TRACEFREE_CLASS"
    14	    "__NOT_DERIVED_OR_ADOPTED"
    15	)
    16	DIMENSION = 4
    17	SYMMETRIC_INDICES = tuple((i, j) for i in range(DIMENSION) for j in range(i, DIMENSION))
    18	PAIRING_WEIGHTS = tuple(
    19	    (1 if i == j else 2) * (-1 if (i == 0) ^ (j == 0) else 1)
    20	    for i, j in SYMMETRIC_INDICES
    21	)
    22	
    23	
    24	def matrix(rows: int, columns: int, fill: Fraction = Fraction(0)) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    25	    return [[fill for _ in range(columns)] for _ in range(rows)]
    26	
    27	
    28	def identity(size: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    29	    result = matrix(size, size)
    30	    for i in range(size):
    31	        result[i][i] = Fraction(1)
    32	    return result
    33	
    34	
    35	def diagonal(*entries: int | Fraction) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    36	    result = matrix(len(entries), len(entries))
    37	    for i, entry in enumerate(entries):
    38	        result[i][i] = Fraction(entry)
    39	    return result
    40	
    41	
    42	def transpose(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    43	    return [list(row) for row in zip(*value)]
    44	
    45	
    46	def multiply(left: list[list[Fraction]], right: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    47	    return [
    48	        [sum((left[i][k] * right[k][j] for k in range(len(right))), Fraction(0))
    49	         for j in range(len(right[0]))]
    50	        for i in range(len(left))
    51	    ]
    52	
    53	
    54	def same(left: list[list[Fraction]], right: list[list[Fraction]]) -> bool:
    55	    return left == right
    56	
    57	
    58	def matrix_key(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> tuple[Fraction, ...]:
    59	    return tuple(entry for row in value for entry in row)
    60	
    61	
    62	def symmetric_vector(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[Fraction]:
    63	    return [value[i][j] for i, j in SYMMETRIC_INDICES]
    64	
    65	
    66	def rank(rows: list[list[Fraction]]) -> int:
    67	    work = [list(row) for row in rows if any(row)]
    68	    if not work:
    69	        return 0
    70	    row_index = 0
    71	    for column in range(len(work[0])):
    72	        pivot = next((i for i in range(row_index, len(work)) if work[i][column]), None)
    73	        if pivot is None:
    74	            continue
    75	        work[row_index], work[pivot] = work[pivot], work[row_index]
    76	        scale = work[row_index][column]
    77	        work[row_index] = [entry / scale for entry in work[row_index]]
    78	        for i in range(len(work)):
    79	            if i == row_index or not work[i][column]:
    80	                continue
    81	            factor = work[i][column]
    82	            work[i] = [a - factor * b for a, b in zip(work[i], work[row_index])]
    83	        row_index += 1
    84	        if row_index == len(work):
    85	            break
    86	    return row_index
    87	
    88	
    89	def nullspace(rows: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    90	    work = [list(row) for row in rows]
    91	    if not work:
    92	        return []
    93	    pivot_columns: list[int] = []
    94	    row_index = 0
    95	    for column in range(len(work[0])):
    96	        pivot = next((i for i in range(row_index, len(work)) if work[i][column]), None)
    97	        if pivot is None:
    98	            continue
    99	        work[row_index], work[pivot] = work[pivot], work[row_index]
   100	        pivot_value = work[row_index][column]
   101	        work[row_index] = [entry / pivot_value for entry in work[row_index]]
   102	        for i in range(len(work)):
   103	            if i == row_index or not work[i][column]:
   104	                continue
   105	            factor = work[i][column]
   106	            work[i] = [a - factor * b for a, b in zip(work[i], work[row_index])]
   107	        pivot_columns.append(column)
   108	        row_index += 1
   109	        if row_index == len(work):
   110	            break
   111	    free_columns = [column for column in range(len(work[0])) if column not in pivot_columns]
   112	    basis: list[list[Fraction]] = []
   113	    for free in free_columns:
   114	        vector = [Fraction(0) for _ in range(len(work[0]))]
   115	        vector[free] = Fraction(1)
   116	        for i, pivot in enumerate(pivot_columns):
   117	            vector[pivot] = -work[i][free]
   118	        basis.append(vector)
   119	    return basis
   120	
   121	
   122	def boost(axis: int, sign: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
   123	    result = identity(DIMENSION)
   124	    c = Fraction(5, 3)
   125	    s = sign * Fraction(4, 3)
   126	    result[0][0] = c
   127	    result[axis][axis] = c
   128	    result[0][axis] = s
   129	    result[axis][0] = s
   130	    return result
   131	
   132	
   133	def rotation(first: int, second: int, sign: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
   134	    result = identity(DIMENSION)
   135	    c = Fraction(3, 5)
   136	    s = sign * Fraction(4, 5)
   137	    result[first][first] = c
   138	    result[second][second] = c
   139	    result[first][second] = -s
   140	    result[second][first] = s
   141	    return result
   142	
   143	
   144	def lorentz_orbit() -> tuple[list[list[Fraction]], list[int], int]:
   145	    eta = diagonal(-1, 1, 1, 1)
   146	    seed = diagonal(1, 1, 0, 0)
   147	    generators = [identity(DIMENSION)]
   148	    generators.extend(boost(axis, sign) for axis in range(1, 4) for sign in (1, -1))
   149	    generators.extend(
   150	        rotation(first, second, sign)
   151	        for first, second in ((1, 2), (1, 3), (2, 3))
   152	        for sign in (1, -1)
   153	    )
   154	    candidates = list(generators)
   155	    candidates.extend(multiply(left, right) for left in generators for right in generators)
   156	    transforms: list[list[list[Fraction]]] = []
   157	    seen: set[tuple[Fraction, ...]] = set()
   158	    for candidate in candidates:
   159	        key = matrix_key(candidate)
   160	        if key not in seen:
   161	            seen.add(key)
   162	            transforms.append(candidate)
   163	
   164	    orbit: list[list[Fraction]] = []
   165	    for transform in transforms:
   166	        assert same(multiply(multiply(transpose(transform), eta), transform), eta)
   167	        tangent = multiply(multiply(transpose(transform), seed), transform)
   168	        assert sum(eta[i][i] * tangent[i][i] for i in range(DIMENSION)) == 0
   169	        orbit.append(symmetric_vector(tangent))
   170	
   171	    selected: list[list[Fraction]] = []
   172	    selected_indices: list[int] = []
   173	    for index, vector in enumerate(orbit):
   174	        if rank(selected + [vector]) > rank(selected):
   175	            selected.append(vector)
   176	            selected_indices.append(index)
   177	        if len(selected) == 9:
   178	            break
   179	    return orbit, selected_indices, len(generators)
   180	
   181	
   182	def pair_row(tangent: list[Fraction]) -> list[Fraction]:
   183	    return [weight * entry for weight, entry in zip(PAIRING_WEIGHTS, tangent)]
   184	
   185	
   186	def proportional(left: list[Fraction], right: list[Fraction]) -> bool:
   187	    ratio: Fraction | None = None
   188	    for a, b in zip(left, right):
   189	        if b == 0:
   190	            if a != 0:
   191	                return False
   192	            continue
   193	        candidate = a / b
   194	        if ratio is None:
   195	            ratio = candidate
   196	        elif candidate != ratio:
   197	            return False
   198	    return ratio is not None
   199	
   200	
   201	def derivative(profile: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]) -> dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]:
   202	    result: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]] = {}
   203	    for power, coefficient in profile.items():
   204	        if power:
   205	            result[power - 1] = tuple(Fraction(power) * item for item in coefficient)
   206	    return result
   207	
   208	
   209	def scaled_shifted(
   210	    profile: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]],
   211	    factor: Fraction,
   212	    shift: int,
   213	) -> dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]:
   214	    return {
   215	        power + shift: tuple(factor * item for item in coefficient)
   216	        for power, coefficient in profile.items()
   217	    }
   218	
   219	
   220	def add_profiles(*profiles: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]) -> dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]]:
   221	    result: dict[int, tuple[Fraction, Fraction, Fraction]] = {}
   222	    for profile in profiles:
   223	        for power, coefficient in profile.items():
   224	            previous = result.get(power, (Fraction(0), Fraction(0), Fraction(0)))
   225	            value = tuple(a + b for a, b in zip(previous, coefficient))
   226	            if any(value):
   227	                result[power] = value
   228	            elif power in result:
   229	                del result[power]
   230	    return result
   231

    12	def zero_matrix() -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    13	    return [[Fraction(0) for _ in range(4)] for _ in range(4)]
    14	
    15	
    16	def covector(vector: list[Fraction]) -> list[Fraction]:
    17	    return [-vector[0], vector[1], vector[2], vector[3]]
    18	
    19	
    20	def outer(left: list[Fraction], right: list[Fraction]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    21	    return [[left[i] * right[j] for j in range(4)] for i in range(4)]
    22	
    23	
    24	def add(*values: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    25	    return [[sum((value[i][j] for value in values), Fraction(0)) for j in range(4)] for i in range(4)]
    26	
    27	
    28	def scale(factor: Fraction, value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    29	    return [[factor * entry for entry in row] for row in value]
    30	
    31	
    32	def subtract(left: list[list[Fraction]], right: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    33	    return add(left, scale(Fraction(-1), right))
    34	
    35	
    36	def pair_tangent(u: list[Fraction], n: list[Fraction]) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    37	    u_flat = covector(u)
    38	    n_flat = covector(n)
    39	    return add(outer(u_flat, u_flat), outer(n_flat, n_flat))
    40	
    41	
    42	def inner(left: list[Fraction], right: list[Fraction]) -> Fraction:
    43	    return -left[0] * right[0] + sum(left[i] * right[i] for i in range(1, 4))
    44	
    45	
    46	def vectorize(value: list[list[Fraction]]) -> list[Fraction]:
    47	    return [value[i][j] for i in range(4) for j in range(i, 4)]
    48	
    49	
    50	def rank(rows: list[list[Fraction]]) -> int:
    51	    work = [list(row) for row in rows if any(row)]
    52	    row_index = 0
    53	    for column in range(len(work[0]) if work else 0):
    54	        pivot = next((i for i in range(row_index, len(work)) if work[i][column]), None)
    55	        if pivot is None:
    56	            continue
    57	        work[row_index], work[pivot] = work[pivot], work[row_index]
    58	        divisor = work[row_index][column]
    59	        work[row_index] = [entry / divisor for entry in work[row_index]]
    60	        for i in range(row_index + 1, len(work)):
    61	            if work[i][column]:
    62	                factor = work[i][column]
    63	                work[i] = [a - factor * b for a, b in zip(work[i], work[row_index])]
    64	        row_index += 1
    65	    return row_index
    66	
    67	
    68	def basis_matrix(i: int, j: int) -> list[list[Fraction]]:
    69	    result = zero_matrix()
    70	    result[i][j] = Fraction(1)
    71	    result[j][i] = Fraction(1)
    72	    return result
    73	
    74	
    75	def build_result() -> dict[str, object]:
    76	    checks = 0
    77	    basis = [[Fraction(1 if i == j else 0) for i in range(4)] for j in range(4)]
    78	    e0, e1, e2, e3 = basis
    79	    spatial = (e1, e2, e3)
    80	    diagonal_tangents = [pair_tangent(e0, direction) for direction in spatial]
    81	    for i, tangent in enumerate(diagonal_tangents, start=1):
    82	        assert inner(e0, e0) == -1
    83	        assert inner(spatial[i - 1], spatial[i - 1]) == 1
    84	        assert vectorize(tangent) == vectorize(pair_tangent(e0, spatial[i - 1]))
    85	        checks += 3
    86	
    87	    spatial_cross: list[list[list[Fraction]]] = []
    88	    c = Fraction(5, 13)
    89	    s = Fraction(12, 13)
    90	    assert c * c + s * s == 1
    91	    checks += 1
    92	    for first, second in ((0, 1), (0, 2), (1, 2)):
    93	        n = [c * spatial[first][k] + s * spatial[second][k] for k in range(4)]
    94	        assert inner(n, n) == 1 and inner(e0, n) == 0
    95	        mixed = pair_tangent(e0, n)
    96	        residual = subtract(
    97	            subtract(mixed, scale(c * c, diagonal_tangents[first])),
    98	            scale(s * s, diagonal_tangents[second]),
    99	        )
   100	        expected = scale(c * s, basis_matrix(first + 1, second + 1))
   101	        assert residual == expected
   102	        spatial_cross.append(residual)
   103	        checks += 2
   104	
   105	    time_cross: list[list[list[Fraction]]] = []
   106	    ch = Fraction(5, 4)
   107	    sh = Fraction(3, 4)
   108	    assert ch * ch - sh * sh == 1
   109	    checks += 1
   110	    for index, direction in enumerate(spatial):
   111	        u = [ch * e0[k] + sh * direction[k] for k in range(4)]
   112	        n = [sh * e0[k] + ch * direction[k] for k in range(4)]
   113	        assert inner(u, u) == -1 and inner(n, n) == 1 and inner(u, n) == 0
   114	        residual = subtract(pair_tangent(u, n), scale(ch * ch + sh * sh, diagonal_tangents[index]))
   115	        expected = scale(-2 * ch * sh, basis_matrix(0, index + 1))
   116	        assert residual == expected
   117	        time_cross.append(residual)
   118	        checks += 2
   119	
   120	    constructive_basis = diagonal_tangents + spatial_cross + time_cross
   121	    assert rank([vectorize(value) for value in constructive_basis]) == 9
   122	    checks += 1
   123	
   124	    # Direct annihilator equations: A_ii=-A_00, A_ij=0, A_0i=0.
   125	    free = Fraction(7, 5)
   126	    response = zero_matrix()
   127	    response[0][0] = free
   128	    for i in range(1, 4):
   129	        response[i][i] = -free
   130	    metric_multiple = [[Fraction(0) for _ in range(4)] for _ in range(4)]
   131	    metric_multiple[0][0] = free
   132	    for i in range(1, 4):
   133	        metric_multiple[i][i] = -free
   134	    assert response == metric_multiple
   135	    checks += 1
   136	
   137	    # The untouched trace is not fixed: two distinct nonzero metric multiples obey all balances.
   138	    assert scale(Fraction(2), metric_multiple) != scale(Fraction(3), metric_multiple)
   139	    checks += 1
   140	
   141	    return {
   142	        "status": "PASS",
   143	        "method": "constructive_plane_combinations_with_distinct_rational_rotation_and_boost",
   144	        "constructive_shape_rank": 9,
   145	        "annihilator_equations": "A_ii=-A_00; A_ij=0; A_0i=0",
   146	        "annihilator": "span(g_ab)",
   147	        "common_trace_magnitude_fixed": False,
   148	        "independent_checks": checks,
   149	    }
   150	
   151	
   152	def main() -> None:
   153	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
   154	    parser.add_argument("--output", type=Path)
   155	    args = parser.parse_args()

   158	    if args.output:

     1	# UDT founding-metric value-law problem
     2	
     3	Date: 2026-08-25
     4	
     5	Purpose: self-contained cold-solver brief for an external mathematical LLM
     6	Status: problem statement, not canon and not a request to confirm UDT
     7	
     8	## Executive statement
     9	
    10	Universal Dimensionality Theory (UDT) has a short founded reciprocal construction and a much
    11	larger, externally audited geometric evaluator around it. The present problem is narrower than
    12	“choose one physical metric from every Lorentz metric.” In the bounded primary branch, the metric
    13	already has the form
    14	
    15	\[
    16	g_\phi=-e^{-2\phi(r)}c_E^2dt^2+e^{2\phi(r)}dr^2+r^2d\Omega^2,
    17	\]
    18	
    19	and its observer-pair reciprocal kernel is derived algebraically after a physical pair pullback is
    20	supplied. Angular, screen, and mixing contributions enter the pullback before the terminal pair
    21	readout. There is no independent response profile to bolt onto the metric.
    22	
    23	The unresolved object is the valued score itself: what fixes or propagates the function \(\phi\)
    24	across physical separation and, eventually, across time? Existing identities reconstruct supplied
    25	values and enforce consistency, but do not generate the values. The current bounded evidence does
    26	not define an ODE, PDE, or GPU initial-value problem.
    27	
    28	Your task is to determine whether a nonidentity value law follows from the stated founding
    29	structure but has been overlooked. A valid answer may be:
    30	
    31	1. a derivation of a native local or invariant metric condition;
    32	2. a derivation of a genuinely global completed-relation law;
    33	3. a proof that the stated premises underdetermine the values, together with the smallest exact
    34	   additional premise type that would close them; or
    35	4. identification of an inconsistency in the chain.
    36	
    37	Do not assume that a conventional field equation must exist. An algebraic finite-family theorem,
    38	a functional equation, an integrability condition, or a global relation constraint could be the
    39	answer. Conversely, do not invent a law merely because prediction is desired.
    40	
    41	## 1. Epistemic vocabulary
    42	
    43	The status words below are part of the mathematical specification.
    44	
    45	| Label | Meaning |
    46	|---|---|
    47	| `OBSERVED` | Empirical calibration input, not derived theory. |
    48	| `DERIVED` | Follows from the declared premises in the stated scope. |
    49	| `DERIVED_CONDITIONAL` | Follows after explicitly supplied metric, query, germ, or working premise. |
    50	| `WORKING` | Provisional frame retained for investigation; not canon. |
    51	| `WORKING_FOUNDATIONAL_CLARIFICATION` | Load-bearing provisional interpretation adopted by Charles Rotter, not derived from the bare metric. |
    52	| `POSIT` | Additional declared assumption. |
    53	| `CHOSE` | Convention, control family, or freely selected structure. |
    54	| `OPEN` | Not presently owned by the theory. |
    55	
    56	The strongest current statements are externally verified with caveats in bounded source and
    57	geometric regimes. None has been canonized merely by appearing here.
    58	
    59	## 2. Founding premises
    60	
    61	### F1. Reciprocal \(c_E\) identity
    62	
    63	`OBSERVED` calibration plus proposed foundational interpretation:
    64	
    65	\[
    66	L=c_ET,
    67	\qquad
    68	T=\frac{L}{c_E}.
    69	\]
    70	
    71	The two directions are coequal clock/ruler conversions. The constant \(c_E\) has dimensions of
    72	length per time. It is not, by itself, a distance, an area, a history equation, or a proof about
    73	local signal propagation.
    74	
    75	### F2. Dual Reciprocity
    76	
    77	Foundational UDT interpretation: a positional comparison acts contragrediently on the two sides of
    78	the clock/ruler conversion pair. With
    79	
    80	\[
    81	q=\begin{pmatrix}c_Edt\\dr\end{pmatrix},
    82	\qquad
    83	P(\Delta)=\begin{pmatrix}u(\Delta)&0\\0&v(\Delta)\end{pmatrix},
    84	\qquad
    85	K=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix},
    86	\]
    87	
    88	Dual Reciprocity is
    89	
    90	\[
    91	P(\Delta)^T K P(\Delta)=K.
    92	\]
    93	
    94	Therefore
    95	
    96	\[
    97	u(\Delta)v(\Delta)=1.
    98	\]
    99	
   100	This conclusion uses Dual Reciprocity. Ordinary covariance of the conversion map would instead
   101	give \(u=v\), so the inverse squeeze does not follow from the numerical existence of \(c_E\) alone.
   102	
   103	### F3. Positional composition and reversal
   104	
   105	`POSIT` / positional-relativity premise:
   106	
   107	\[
   108	P(\Delta_1+\Delta_2)=P(\Delta_1)P(\Delta_2),
   109	\qquad
   110	P(-\Delta)=P(\Delta)^{-1}.
   111	\]
   112	
   113	Continuity or measurability, positivity, and at least one nonidentity comparison give, after choosing
   114	the sign and unit of the additive coordinate,
   115	
   116	\[
   117	D(\delta)=\operatorname{diag}(e^{-\delta},e^{+\delta}).
   118	\]
   119	
   120	The nontriviality input is separate: the trivial representation also obeys the group laws.
   121	
   122	### F4. Local metric readout
   123	
   124	`DECLARED READOUT` / local Lorentzian continuity: the transformed clock and ruler coframe is read
   125	quadratically, and the static spherical chart uses an areal angular sector. This produces the
   126	primary metric in Section 3.
   127	
   128	### W1. Completed-pair Dual Reciprocity
   129	
   130	`WORKING_FOUNDATIONAL_CLARIFICATION`, not canon: Dual Reciprocity applies to the completed physical
   131	observer-pair pullback only after angular, screen, shift, and mixing contributions have entered.
   132	Arbitrarily calibrated pair curves remain useful controls, but are not rival physical kernels.
   133	
   134	### W2. Co-presence

   141	### W3. UDT--GR quiet-limit reduction
   142	
   143	`WORKING/POSIT`, provisionally authorized by Charles Rotter on 2026-08-25, not canon: UDT is a
   144	metric-native extension of GR by a finite-separation reciprocal channel. In a quiet intermediate
   145	regime, the complete UDT law must reduce continuously to the corresponding GR field structure,
   146	constraint propagation, causal structure, and observer predictions. Departures at the extreme
   147	regimes must arise from the same complete reciprocal metric machinery, not from a fitted regime
   148	window or a correction applied after pair readout.
   149	
   150	This is a reduction requirement, not a derivation of the Einstein equations from F1--F4 or W1. It
   151	does not yet define the invariant quietness parameter, the full UDT parent operator, the source
   152	sector, or the law governing departure from the GR branch. In the bounded first audit, GR vacuum
   153	equations may be used only as an explicitly imported comparison condition on the static spherical
   154	exterior.
   155	
   156	### W3.1. Full-metric quiet non-discard theorem
   157	
   158	`DERIVED_CONDITIONAL`, bounded to the primary static-spherical positive-
   159	\(f=e^{-2\phi}\) W3 comparator: the quiet comparison must be evaluated with the complete
   160	four-metric, including the areal sphere. Its two independent diagonal residuals are
   161	
   162	\[
   163	\mathcal E_0=r f'+f-1,
   164	\qquad
   165	\mathcal E_1=r f'+\frac{r^2}{2}f'',
   166	\]
   167	
   168	and the native G201 angular amplitudes obey the exact identity
   169	
   170	\[
   171	A_\parallel+A_\perp=\mathcal E_1-\mathcal E_0.
   172	\]
   173	
   174	On the imported quiet-vacuum comparison family \(f=1+C/r\), with \(C\ne0\),
   175	
   176	\[
   177	A_\parallel=\frac{3C}{2r},
   178	\qquad
   179	A_\perp=-\frac{3C}{2r}.
   180	\]
   181	
   182	Thus the angular instruments are active and cancel; they were not turned off to recover the GR
   183	limit. The isolated two-dimensional clock-radius metric has identically zero Einstein tensor for
   184	every \(f\), so it is a vacuous substitute. Flattening the areal sphere instead gives an exact unit
   185	residual on \(f=1+C/r\). Either shortcut corrupts the bounded comparison.
   186	
   187	This theorem does not select the UDT parent law. In particular, zero angular trace alone retains
   188	
   189	\[
   190	f=1+a r^2+\frac{b}{r},
   191	\qquad
   192	\mathcal E_0=\mathcal E_1=3a r^2,
   193	\]
   194	
   195	and the imported vacuum condition separately removes \(a\). G260 therefore closes the angular-
   196	discard concern while leaving the parent/source value law, time-live extension, and loud-regime
   197	departure `OPEN`.

That is a documentation/evidence defect, not a refutation of the bounded theorem.
